=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and two integers, k and limit. Your task is to find a non-empty subsequence of nums that:

Has an alternating sum equal to k.
Maximizes the product of all its numbers without the product exceeding limit.

Return the product of the numbers in such a subsequence. If no subsequence satisfies the requirements, return -1.
The alternating sum of a 0-indexed array is defined as the sum of the elements at even indices minus the sum of the elements at odd indices.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,2,3], k = 2, limit = 10
Output: 6
Explanation:
The subsequences with an alternating sum of 2 are:

[1, 2, 3]

Alternating Sum: 1 - 2 + 3 = 2
Product: 1 * 2 * 3 = 6


[2]

Alternating Sum: 2
Product: 2



The maximum product within the limit is 6.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [0,2,3], k = -5, limit = 12
Output: -1
Explanation:
A subsequence with an alternating sum of exactly -5 does not exist.

Example 3:

Input: nums = [2,2,3,3], k = 0, limit = 9
Output: 9
Explanation:
The subsequences with an alternating sum of 0 are:

[2, 2]

Alternating Sum: 2 - 2 = 0
Product: 2 * 2 = 4


[3, 3]

Alternating Sum: 3 - 3 = 0
Product: 3 * 3 = 9


[2, 2, 3, 3]

Alternating Sum: 2 - 2 + 3 - 3 = 0
Product: 2 * 2 * 3 * 3 = 36



The subsequence [2, 2, 3, 3] has the greatest product with an alternating sum equal to k, but 36 > 9. The next greatest product is 9, which is within the limit.

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 150
0 <= nums[i] <= 12
-10^5 <= k <= 10^5
1 <= limit <= 5000

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, nums: List[int], k: int, limit: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:
A0=A1=B0=B1=0

for x in nums:
    # old states
    oA0,oA1,oB0,oB1 = A0,A1,B0,B1
    # skip: keep old states (we can start new_dp as old)
    nA0,nA1,nB0,nB1 = oA0,oA1,oB0,oB1
    # start new subsequence with x
    if x == 0:
        nB1 |= 1 << OFFSET  # sum 0, length1 odd
    else:
        nA1 |= 1 << (x + OFFSET)
    # append x to old states
    # For each old category and parity:
    # parity 0 -> sign +, shift left x; parity 1 -> sign -, shift right x.
    # new category: if old cat A and x==0 -> B, else same cat; old cat B -> B.
    if x == 0:
        # shift amount 0, but sign? contribution 0, so no sum change, parity flips.
        # From A0 (even, no zero) append 0 -> B1, sum same
        nB1 |= oA0
        # From A1 append 0 -> B0
        nB0 |= oA1
        # From B0 append 0 -> B1
        nB1 |= oB0
        # From B1 append 0 -> B0
        nB0 |= oB1
        # Starting new zero already added; but note append zero to empty? start handles.
    else:
        # x>0
        # From A0 append x -> A1, sum +x
        nA1 |= oA0 << x
        # From A1 append x -> A0, sum -x
        nA0 |= oA1 >> x
        # From B0 append x -> B1, sum +x
        nB1 |= oB0 << x
        # From B1 append x -> B0, sum -x
        nB0 |= oB1 >> x
    A0,A1,B0,B1 = nA0,nA1,nB0,nB1

At end zero_valid = (B0 | B1) & (1 << (k+OFFSET)) != 0.

Let's plan code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, nums: List[int], k: int, limit: int) -> int:
        total = sum(nums)
        if abs(k) > total:
            return -1

        OFFSET = 2000
        target = k + OFFSET
        target_bit = 1 << target

        # positive DP
        dp0 = [0] * (limit + 1)
        dp1 = [0] * (limit + 1)

        for x in nums:
            if x == 0 or x > limit:
                continue
            new0 = dp0[:]  # skip
            new1 = dp1[:]
            max_p = limit // x
            # append x
            for p in range(1, max_p + 1):
                bits = dp0[p]
                if bits:
                    new1[p * x] |= bits << x
                bits = dp1[p]
                if bits:
                    new0[p * x] |= bits >> x
            # start new subsequence
            new1[x] |= 1 << (x + OFFSET)
            dp0, dp1 = new0, new1

        for p in range(limit, 0, -1):
            if (dp0[p] | dp1[p]) & target_bit:
                return p

        # zero-product DP
        A0 = A1 = B0 = B1 = 0
        for x in nums:
            oA0, oA1, oB0, oB1 = A0, A1, B0, B1
            nA0, nA1, nB0, nB1 = oA0, oA1, oB0, oB1
            if x == 0:
                # append 0
                nB1 |= oA0  # even no-zero -> odd with zero
                nB0 |= oA1  # odd no-zero -> even with zero
                nB1 |= oB0  # even with zero -> odd with zero
                nB0 |= oB1  # odd with zero -> even with zero
                # start [0]
                nB1 |= 1 << OFFSET
            else:
                nA1 |= oA0 << x
                nA0 |= oA1 >> x
                nB1 |= oB0 << x
                nB0 |= oB1 >> x
                nA1 |= 1 << (x + OFFSET)
            A0, A1, B0, B1 = nA0, nA1, nB0, nB1

        if (B0 | B1) & target_bit:
            return 0
        return -1

Need verify zero DP with x==0: We set nB1 initially oB1 (skip old B1). Then nB1 |= oA0, oB0, start. nB0 initially oB0. Then nB0 |= oA1, oB1. Good. But note `nB0` initial oB0, and then `nB1 |= oB0` uses oB0; no issue. `nB0 |= oB1` uses oB1. Good.

Now, let's think about if zero DP with x>0 and old B0/B1: zero. Good.

Now, let's think about if zero DP with x==0 and old A0/A1: zero. Good.

Now, let's think about if zero
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, nums: List[int], k: int, limit: int) -> int:
        total = sum(nums)
        if abs(k) > total:
            return -1

        OFFSET = 2000
        target = k + OFFSET
        target_bit = 1 << target

        # DP for positive products.
        # dp0[p]: even length subsequences with product p, bitset of alternating sums
        # dp1[p]: odd length subsequences with product p, bitset of alternating sums
        dp0 = [0] * (limit + 1)
        dp1 = [0] * (limit + 1)

        for x in nums:
            if x == 0 or x > limit:
                continue

            new0 = dp0[:]  # skip current number
            new1 = dp1[:]

            max_p = limit // x

            # Append x to existing subsequences.
            for p in range(1, max_p + 1):
                bits = dp0[p]
                if bits:
                    new1[p * x] |= bits << x

                bits = dp1[p]
                if bits:
                    new0[p * x] |= bits >> x

            # Start a new subsequence consisting of only x.
            new1[x] |= 1 << (x + OFFSET)

            dp0, dp1 = new0, new1

        # Best positive product.
        for p in range(limit, 0, -1):
            if (dp0[p] | dp1[p]) & target_bit:
                return p

        # DP for zero products: a subsequence has product 0 iff it contains a zero.
        # A: no zero selected, B: at least one zero selected.
        # Suffix 0/1 = length parity.
        A0 = A1 = B0 = B1 = 0

        for x in nums:
            oA0, oA1, oB0, oB1 = A0, A1, B0, B1

            nA0, nA1, nB0, nB1 = oA0, oA1, oB0, oB1

            if x == 0:
                # Append 0: sum unchanged, parity flips.
                nB1 |= oA0
                nB0 |= oA1
                nB1 |= oB0
                nB0 |= oB1

                # Start subsequence [0].
                nB1 |= 1 << OFFSET
            else:
                # Append positive x.
                nA1 |= oA0 << x
                nA0 |= oA1 >> x
                nB1 |= oB0 << x
                nB0 |= oB1 >> x

                # Start subsequence [x].
                nA1 |= 1 << (x + OFFSET)

            A0, A1, B0, B1 = nA0, nA1, nB0, nB1

        if (B0 | B1) & target_bit:
            return 0

        return -1
```
